@ file mesh.sh, last written 2026-10-04T23:56:48Z
#!/bin/bash
# mesh.sh SIZE DEPTH FLAG TRIALS TAG [MODE [STALL]]
# One mesh-p 3 2 DEPTH. Each trial: seats 0,1,2 each write one message,
# released at once off a fifo barrier: printf length line, SEAT line, SIZE
# bytes of a/b/c, piped into dd bs=4096 FLAG. p reads the whole stream
# (exactly what three messages total) off its one read end, bounded, and
# an.py parses it as p would: length line, SEAT line, head -c length.
# MODE keep: every seat drains its read ends all along (dd, its own process
# per read end). MODE stall: seat 2 drains nothing until STALL seconds
# after the release, then drains until the trial ends.
set -u
nSize=$1; nDepth=$2; osFlag=$3; nTrials=$4; osTag=$5; eMode=${6:-keep}; nStall=${7:-1}
[ "$osFlag" = none ] && osFlag=
H=$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)
S=/home/user/ICC/.claude/skills
W=$(mktemp -d "$H/$osTag.XXXXXX")
pLog=$H/$osTag.log
aPids=()
pPatch=
fCleanup() {
  [ ${#aPids[@]} -eq 0 ] || kill "${aPids[@]}" 2>/dev/null
  wait 2>/dev/null
  [ -z "$pPatch" ] || "$S/icc-patch/scripts/remove" "$pPatch"
  echo "removed $pPatch" >> "$pLog"
}
trap fCleanup EXIT
trap 'exit 1' INT TERM HUP
pPatch=$(timeout 60 "$S/icc-patch/scripts/create" mesh-p 3 2 "$nDepth") || { echo "create failed" >> "$pLog"; exit 1; }
echo "$pPatch" >> "$H/patches.made"
cp "$pPatch/patch" "$W/map"; cp "$pPatch/made" "$W/made"; cat "$W/made" >> "$H/all.made"
aW=(); for i in 0 1 2; do aW[i]=$(awk -v s=$i '$1==s && $2==0 {print $3}' "$W/map"); done
pP=$(awk '$1=="p" && $2==1 {print $3}' "$W/map")
aBody=(); for i in 0 1 2; do aBody[i]=$(head -c "$nSize" /dev/zero | tr '\0' "$(printf '\\%03o' $((97 + i)))"); done
nTotal=0; for i in 0 1 2; do nTotal=$((nTotal + ${#nSize} + 1 + 7 + nSize)); done
fDrain() { # seat i's read ends, drained until killed (bounded at 7200 s)
  local r
  for r in $(awk -v s=$1 '$1==s && $2==1 {print $3}' "$W/map"); do
    timeout 7200 dd if="$r/0" of=/dev/null bs=65536 status=none &
    aPids+=($!)
  done
}
fDrain 0; fDrain 1
[ "$eMode" = stall ] || fDrain 2
mkfifo "$W/go"
exec {fdGo}<>"$W/go"
echo "# $osTag size=$nSize depth=$nDepth flag='$osFlag' trials=$nTrials mode=$eMode stall=$nStall total=$nTotal patch=$pPatch load=$(cut -d' ' -f1-3 /proc/loadavg)" >> "$pLog"
for ((t = 1; t <= nTrials; t++)); do
  aWr=()
  for i in 0 1 2; do
    (
      read -r -N 1 -t 30 _ < "$W/go" || exit 3
      printf '%s\nSEAT %s\n%s' "$nSize" "$i" "${aBody[i]}" |
        timeout 60 dd of="${aW[i]}/0" bs=4096 $osFlag status=none
    ) &
    aWr+=($!)
  done
  timeout 60 head -c "$nTotal" < "$pP/0" > "$W/raw" &
  pidP=$!
  pidS2=
  sleep 0.2
  t0=$EPOCHREALTIME
  if [ "$eMode" = stall ]; then
    printf xxx >&"$fdGo"
    ( sleep "$nStall"; aR=$(awk '$1==2 && $2==1 {print $3}' "$W/map")
      for r in $aR; do timeout 120 dd if="$r/0" of=/dev/null bs=65536 status=none & done; wait ) &
    pidS2=$!
  else
    printf xxx >&"$fdGo"
  fi
  rcW=; for w in "${aWr[@]}"; do wait "$w"; rcW=$rcW$?; done
  wait "$pidP"; rcP=$?
  t1=$EPOCHREALTIME
  if [ -n "$pidS2" ]; then
    # let seat 2 finish draining, then stop it
    sleep 0.3; pkill -P "$pidS2" 2>/dev/null; kill "$pidS2" 2>/dev/null; wait "$pidS2" 2>/dev/null
    for r in $(awk '$1==2 && $2==1 {print $3}' "$W/map"); do timeout 0.3 dd if="$r/0" of=/dev/null bs=65536 status=none; done
  fi
  osRes=$("$H/an.py" "$W/raw" "$nSize" "$nTotal")
  nLeft=0
  case $osRes in
    OK*) ;;
    *) nLeft=$(timeout 1 dd if="$pP/0" bs=65536 status=none | wc -c)
       cp "$W/raw" "$W/raw.bad.$t" ;;
  esac
  printf '%s t=%d rcW=%s rcP=%s ms=%d left=%s %s\n' "$osTag" "$t" "$rcW" "$rcP" \
    $(( (${t1/./} - ${t0/./}) / 1000 )) "$nLeft" "$osRes" >> "$pLog"
done
exec {fdGo}>&-
@ file an.py, last written 2026-10-04T23:55:45Z
#!/usr/bin/python3
# an.py RAW SIZE TOTAL: classify what p read off its one read end.
# p's procedure, on the bytes it read: a length line, a SEAT line, then
# that many bytes; three times. Bodies are a/b/c for seats 0/1/2.
import re, sys
raw = open(sys.argv[1], 'rb').read()
size = int(sys.argv[2]); total = int(sys.argv[3])
runs = [(m.group()[:1], len(m.group())) for m in re.finditer(rb'a+|b+|c+|[^abc]+', raw)]
summ = ' '.join(('h' if k not in (b'a', b'b', b'c') else k.decode()) + str(n) for k, n in runs)
inter = desync = False
pos = 0; order = ''
for k in range(3):
    nl = raw.find(b'\n', pos)
    line = raw[pos:nl] if nl >= 0 else b''
    if nl < 0 or not line.isdigit():
        desync = True; break
    L = int(line); pos = nl + 1
    nl = raw.find(b'\n', pos)
    seat = raw[pos:nl] if nl >= 0 else b''
    m = re.fullmatch(rb'SEAT ([012])', seat)
    if nl < 0 or not m:
        desync = True; break
    i = int(m.group(1)); pos = nl + 1
    body = raw[pos:pos + L]; pos += L
    order += str(i)
    if len(body) < L or L != size:
        desync = True; break
    if set(body) != {ord('abc'[i])}:
        inter = True
import itertools
exp = [b'%d\nSEAT %d\n' % (size, i) + b'abc'[i:i+1] * size for i in range(3)]
clean = any(raw == exp[a] + exp[b] + exp[c] for a, b, c in itertools.permutations(range(3)))
if len(raw) < total:
    cls = 'HANG'
elif not clean:
    cls = 'INTERLEAVE' if inter else 'MIXED'
elif inter:
    cls = 'INTERLEAVE'
elif desync:
    cls = 'DESYNC'
elif len(runs) == 6 and sorted(order) == ['0', '1', '2']:
    cls = 'OK'
else:
    cls = 'ODD'
print(cls, len(raw), len(runs), order, 'pparse=' + ('interleave' if inter else 'desync' if desync else 'whole'), summ if cls != 'OK' else '')

#!/bin/bash
# direct.sh SIZE FLAG TRIALS TAG: control with no tee. Three pipes, each the
# inlet of one merge, written straight by a seat's printf | dd; p reads the
# merge's outlet. Same messages, barrier and analyzer as mesh.sh.
set -u
nSize=$1; osFlag=$2; nTrials=$3; osTag=$4
[ "$osFlag" = none ] && osFlag=
H=$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)
S=/home/user/ICC/.claude/skills
W=$(mktemp -d "$H/$osTag.XXXXXX")
pLog=$H/$osTag.log
aIn=(); pOut=; pMerge=
fCleanup() {
  [ -z "$pMerge" ] || "$S/icc-merge/scripts/remove" "$pMerge"
  for p in "${aIn[@]}" $pOut; do "$S/icc-pipes/scripts/remove" "$p"; done
  echo "removed $pMerge ${aIn[*]} $pOut" >> "$pLog"
}
trap fCleanup EXIT
trap 'exit 1' INT TERM HUP
for i in 0 1 2; do aIn[i]=$("$S/icc-pipes/scripts/create" 2) || exit 1; done
pOut=$("$S/icc-pipes/scripts/create" 2) || exit 1
pMerge=$(timeout 30 "$S/icc-merge/scripts/create" "$pOut" 0 "${aIn[@]}") || exit 1
echo "$pMerge ${aIn[*]} $pOut" >> "$H/direct.made"
aBody=(); for i in 0 1 2; do aBody[i]=$(head -c "$nSize" /dev/zero | tr '\0' "$(printf '\\%03o' $((97 + i)))"); done
nTotal=0; for i in 0 1 2; do nTotal=$((nTotal + ${#nSize} + 1 + 7 + nSize)); done
mkfifo "$W/go"
exec {fdGo}<>"$W/go"
echo "# $osTag size=$nSize flag='$osFlag' trials=$nTrials total=$nTotal merge=$pMerge load=$(cut -d' ' -f1-3 /proc/loadavg)" >> "$pLog"
for ((t = 1; t <= nTrials; t++)); do
  aWr=()
  for i in 0 1 2; do
    (
      read -r -N 1 -t 30 _ < "$W/go" || exit 3
      printf '%s\nSEAT %s\n%s' "$nSize" "$i" "${aBody[i]}" |
        timeout 60 dd of="${aIn[i]}/0" bs=4096 $osFlag status=none
    ) &
    aWr+=($!)
  done
  timeout 60 head -c "$nTotal" < "$pOut/0" > "$W/raw" &
  pidP=$!
  sleep 0.2
  t0=$EPOCHREALTIME
  printf xxx >&"$fdGo"
  rcW=; for w in "${aWr[@]}"; do wait "$w"; rcW=$rcW$?; done
  wait "$pidP"; rcP=$?
  t1=$EPOCHREALTIME
  osRes=$("$H/an.py" "$W/raw" "$nSize" "$nTotal")
  nLeft=0
  case $osRes in
    OK*) ;;
    *) nLeft=$(timeout 1 dd if="$pOut/0" bs=65536 status=none | wc -c)
       cp "$W/raw" "$W/raw.bad.$t" ;;
  esac
  printf '%s t=%d rcW=%s rcP=%s ms=%d left=%s %s\n' "$osTag" "$t" "$rcW" "$rcP" \
    $(( (${t1/./} - ${t0/./}) / 1000 )) "$nLeft" "$osRes" >> "$pLog"
done
exec {fdGo}>&-
@ file all.sh, last written 2026-10-04T23:56:48Z
#!/bin/bash
# all.sh LOAD TRIALS: every size, both variants; LOAD 1 runs nproc busy loops throughout.
H=$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)
nLoad=$1; nTrials=$2
aYes=()
trap '[ ${#aYes[@]} -eq 0 ] || kill "${aYes[@]}" 2>/dev/null; wait' EXIT
trap 'exit 1' INT TERM HUP
if [ "$nLoad" = 1 ]; then
  for ((k = 0; k < $(nproc); k++)); do timeout 7200 yes > /dev/null & aYes+=($!); done
  echo "${aYes[@]}" > "$H/yes.pids"
  sleep 1
fi
osL=$([ "$nLoad" = 1 ] && echo load || echo idle)
for spec in 3000:1 8000:1 20000:1 100000:2 400000:6; do
  for v in iflag=fullblock none; do
    vt=$([ "$v" = none ] && echo nofb || echo fb)
    timeout 3000 "$H/mesh.sh" "${spec%:*}" "${spec#*:}" "$v" "$nTrials" "$osL-${spec%:*}-$vt"
  done
done
echo "all done $osL"

@ file stall.sh, last written 2026-10-04T23:58:02Z
#!/bin/bash
# stall.sh TRIALS: seat 2 reads nothing for the first second of each trial.
# 100000 bytes at DEPTH 1 (seat 2's cable from each tee holds 16 blocks, less
# than one message) and at DEPTH 2 (33 blocks, more than one message).
H=$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)
for d in 1 2; do
  for v in iflag=fullblock none; do
    vt=$([ "$v" = none ] && echo nofb || echo fb)
    timeout 3000 "$H/mesh.sh" 100000 "$d" "$v" "$1" "stall-100000-d$d-$vt" stall 1
  done
done
echo "all done stall"
@ file hcheck.sh, last written 2026-10-05T01:09:18Z
#!/bin/bash
# hcheck.sh: the proposed harness check, standalone. Seats 0 and 1 each write
# 100000 bytes on a mesh-p 3 while seat 2 reads nothing; p's first 2 seconds
# hold less than all of the first seat's and some of the other's.
set -eu
H=$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)
S=/home/user/ICC/.claude/skills
pIccPatch=$S/icc-patch/scripts
pWork=$(mktemp -d "$H/hcheck.XXXXXX")
pEnd() { awk -v s="$1" -v i="$2" -v p="$3" '$1==s && $2==i && ("," $4 ",") ~ ("," p ",") {print $3}' "$pDir/patch"; }
pDir=$("$pIccPatch/create" mesh-p 3)
echo "$pDir" >> "$H/hcheck.made"; cut -d' ' -f1 "$pDir/made" >> "$H/hcheck.made"
aJobs=()
for osSeat in 0 1; do
  pWriteEnd=$(pEnd $osSeat 0 p)
  head -c 100000 /dev/zero | tr '\0' "$osSeat" |
    timeout 10 dd of="$pWriteEnd/0" bs=4096 2>/dev/null &
  aJobs+=($!)
done
pReadEnd=$(pEnd 0 1 1)
timeout 10 dd if="$pReadEnd/0" of=/dev/null bs=4096 2>/dev/null &
aJobs+=($!)
pReadEnd=$(pEnd 1 1 0)
timeout 10 dd if="$pReadEnd/0" of=/dev/null bs=4096 2>/dev/null &
aJobs+=($!)
pReadEnd=$(pEnd p 1 0)
timeout 2 head -c 200000 "$pReadEnd/0" > "$pWork/met" || :
osFirst=$(head -c 1 "$pWork/met")
nFirst=$(tr -cd "$osFirst" < "$pWork/met" | wc -c)
nOther=$(tr -d "$osFirst" < "$pWork/met" | wc -c)
echo "first=$osFirst nFirst=$nFirst nOther=$nOther runs=$(tr -s 01 < "$pWork/met" | wc -c)"
rc=0
[ "$nFirst" -lt 100000 ] && [ "$nOther" -gt 0 ] || rc=1
"$pIccPatch/remove" "$pDir"
wait "${aJobs[@]}" || :
exit $rc

@ report the measuring agent, of these runs
question: From the code alone, when can a tee-fed inlet of p's merge go empty partway through one seat's write, and what does the merge do then?
answer: It can go empty after the tee has put a chunk into the inlet and before it puts the next one there. In that gap the inlet holds only the part of the last chunk the merge has not taken yet, and the merge takes at most 4096 bytes per dd. Each next chunk lands only after three things happen: the seat's dd has written more into the write end, the tee's read(8192) has returned, and the tee has written that chunk to /dev/null and to every other seat's cable. Nothing in either copier makes that happen before the merge looks again. If the merge has taken everything when it next looks, `read -t 0` fails, bMore stays 0, and it moves on to the next SRC. Any other inlet that has bytes then gets in. The only thing that is certain: one write of at most 4096 bytes, with nothing ahead of it in the write end, is one tee read and one atomic write into the inlet. It sits in the inlet whole before the merge can see any of it, so it comes out whole. Measured with strace: coreutils 9.4 tee does read(0, 8192), then write(1) (/dev/null), then write(3) and write(4) in argv order, then reads again. bash 5.2.21 printf '%s\nSEAT %s\n%s' writes the length line (6 bytes for 20000) and the SEAT line (7 bytes) as separate writes, then the body in 4096-byte writes. On the 400000-byte idle trials the merge moved 293 blocks in 605-831 ms per trial (median 710), about 2.4 ms per block including up to 50 ms of idle wait.
mechanism: icc-merge/scripts/create:129 goes round the SRCs in order. 132-153 repeat while bMore is set. 137 is `read -t 0 -u "$fdIn" || continue`. 144 takes one `dd bs=4096 count=1` per block. 151 sets bMore=1 only after a block moved. So the merge leaves a SRC the first time a full pass finds every lane of it empty, and 156 waits `read -t 0.05` when nothing has bytes. icc-tee/scripts/create:114 is `exec tee -a -- "${aOuts[@]}" < "$pSrc/$iLane"`. icc-patch/scripts/create:307-319 puts the other seats' cable heads into osOutlets first (308-309) and the merge inlet last (313-314), then calls `vTee "$pWrite" 0 $osOutlets` (319). On ring-p, create:361 is `vTee "$pSendTail" 0 "$pRecvHead" "$pToParentHead"`, so there too the inlet is the last outlet. Tee SKILL.md:66-70 says each chunk goes to every outlet in order before the next is read. Merge SKILL.md:67-78 says empty is all the copier sees. Pipes SKILL.md:63-66 says dd without fullblock writes what each read returned.
method: Read the copier code (lines above). Ran `strace -e trace=read,write tee -a o1 o2 < fifo` with 20000 bytes fed by `dd bs=4096`. Ran `strace -f -ttt -e trace=write` on `printf '%s\nSEAT %s\n%s' N 0 "$body" | dd bs=4096 [iflag=fullblock]` for N = 3000, 20000, 100000 and 400000, 3 runs each. Without strace in the way, the gap from the SEAT-line write to the first body write was 0.08-0.18 ms and did not depend on size. Merge rate is taken from the trial durations logged in idle-400000-*.log.
confidence: high

question: With every seat keeping up and no load added, does p see interleaving on mesh-p 3 2 DEPTH?
answer: No. All 1000 trials came out whole: 100 per size per variant, at sizes 3000, 8000, 20000, 100000 and 400000, with and without iflag=fullblock. No hangs, no desync, and nothing was left on p's read end. Note that "idle" means no load I added. Another session was running ICC tests on the same machine, and loadavg at the start of each run was 0.18 to 2.12.
mechanism: The merge forks a dd and a command substitution for every block, about 2.4 ms each. The seat's dd and the tee move a chunk with a few syscalls. When nothing preempts them, the next chunk is in the inlet before the merge looks again.
method: Script run.nQL8NK/mesh.sh SIZE DEPTH FLAG 100 TAG, driven by all.sh 0 100. Each run does `icc-patch/scripts/create mesh-p 3 2 DEPTH`, with DEPTH from the given formula: 1 for 3000, 8000 and 20000, 2 for 100000, 6 for 400000. Each seat's two read ends were drained for the whole run, one `timeout 7200 dd if=END/0 of=/dev/null bs=65536 status=none` per end. All three seats were released at once by a fifo barrier: each seat runs `read -N 1` and the main shell writes 3 bytes. Each seat then ran `printf '%s\nSEAT %s\n%s' SIZE i BODY | timeout 60 dd of=WRITE_END/0 bs=4096 [iflag=fullblock] status=none`. BODY was a, b or c repeated, not the seat digit, so body bytes could not be confused with header digits. p ran `timeout 60 head -c TOTAL < P/0` into a file. an.py then parsed it the way p would: length line, SEAT line, that many bytes, three times. It also compared the whole stream against the three messages in every order. Each trial was classed as OK, INTERLEAVE, DESYNC or HANG. Each patch was removed with icc-patch remove.
confidence: high

question: With every seat keeping up and nproc busy loops (4 `yes >/dev/null` on 4 CPUs), does p see interleaving?
answer: Once. With iflag=fullblock, 800 of 800 trials came out whole: 100 at each size plus 300 more at 400000. Without it, 1399 of 1400 came out whole: 100 at each size plus 300 more at each of 3000, 20000 and 400000. The cut trial was 400000 bytes without fullblock (load-400000-nofb t=43). p's stream was seat 0's 14 bytes of length and SEAT lines, then seat 1's whole message, then seat 2's whole message, then seat 0's 400000-byte body. p's parse saw the length line followed by another seat's header. No hangs.
mechanism: Without iflag=fullblock, dd passed printf's separate header writes on as a short write. The tee copied them, and the merge took them and found the inlet empty before the first body block arrived (merge create:137, 151). This is a writer pause carried through the tee, which Merge SKILL.md:71-73 describes.
method: all.sh 1 100 and extra.sh 300, both starting `timeout 7200 yes >/dev/null` nproc times (pids saved and killed on EXIT). Same mesh.sh as above. loadavg at run start was 4-6 once the load had settled.
confidence: high

question: Extra condition: four busy loops per CPU (16 `yes` on 4 CPUs). Which sizes interleave?
answer: Out of 1800 trials, 26 were cut. 3000 bytes: 0 of 150 with fullblock and 0 of 150 without. 8000: 3 of 150 with, 4 of 150 without. 20000: 7 of 300 with, 10 of 300 without. 100000: 0 of 150 with, 2 of 150 without. 400000: 0 of 150 with, 0 of 150 without. So 8000 to 100000 bytes were cut 10 of 600 times with fullblock and 16 of 600 without, and 3000 and 400000 bytes were cut none of 600 times. Every one of the 26 cuts came exactly after the writer's first 4096 bytes: the header lines plus 4082 to 4084 body bytes. Another seat's whole message, or both others', came out before the rest of the cut one. No hangs, no stray exit codes, nothing left on p's end.
mechanism: Same window as above, at the first block. dd with fullblock does not close it, since a 4096-byte block still leaves a gap before the next. The code does not explain why 400000 bytes at DEPTH 6 showed no cuts in 300 trials, and I did not trace it.
method: heavy.sh 150 and heavy2.sh 150 ran `for k < 4*nproc: timeout 7200 yes >/dev/null &`, plus the mesh.sh legs of dheavy.sh 150 (20000 bytes). loadavg 15-19. The cut positions were taken from an.py's run summaries in load4x-*.log and mesh4xb-*.log.
confidence: medium

question: Is it the tee? Control with no tee under the same 4x load.
answer: 20000 bytes written by the same printf | dd straight into the three inlets of an icc-merge, with no tee, were cut in 0 of 300 trials (150 with fullblock, 150 without). In the same run, alternating with it, 20000 bytes through the mesh-p's tees were cut in 6 of 300 (2 with fullblock, 4 without). The explanation is an inference I did not trace. A seat's first write wakes its tee, which is blocked in read. The tee copies that one block to the cables and the inlet and blocks again before the seat's dd runs again. Without a tee nothing is woken by dd's write, because the merge polls and is not blocked on the inlet.
mechanism: tee(1) blocks in read(0, 8192) on the seat's write end (icc-tee create:114). Its copy into the inlet is a separate step that comes after the cables (icc-patch create:307-319).
method: run.nQL8NK/direct.sh 20000 FLAG 150 TAG creates 4 pipes with `icc-pipes/scripts/create 2` and runs `icc-merge/scripts/create OUT 0 IN0 IN1 IN2`, with the same barrier, writer, p reader and an.py. It was interleaved with mesh.sh in dheavy.sh under 16 `yes` loops.
confidence: medium

question: Stated failure case: one seat stops reading so its cable from a tee fills. Does p then see interleaving?
answer: Yes, every time once the cable is smaller than the write. At mesh-p 3 2 1, all three seats wrote 100000 bytes at once while seat 2 read nothing for 1 s and then drained. 20 of 20 trials were cut with fullblock and 20 of 20 without. p got the first 61440 or 65536 bytes (header included) of seats 0 and 1, and their rest only after seat 2 read again. Seat 2's own message was whole each time: between the two parts in 38 trials, and first in 2. At DEPTH 2, whose cable holds 33 blocks (135168 bytes, more than one message), 40 of 40 came out whole in about 200 ms, before seat 2 read at all. Separately, I ran the proposed harness check standalone 10 times under 16 busy loops and it passed 10 of 10. In it, p's first 2 s held 65536 bytes of seat 0 and 57344 to 61440 bytes of seat 1.
mechanism: The tee blocks in its write to seat 2's full cable head. That write comes before the inlet in argv (icc-patch create:307-319). So the inlet gets nothing more, the merge drains it, and then it goes on to the next inlet (merge create:137, 151). Patch SKILL.md:178 says a tee waits on its fullest outlet.
method: stall.sh 20 runs `mesh.sh 100000 {1,2} {iflag=fullblock,none} 20 TAG stall 1`. Seat 2's drainers start only `sleep 1` after release and are killed after p finishes; its ends are then emptied with `timeout 0.3 dd`. hcheck.sh was run 10 times.
confidence: high

question: What was not measured
answer: Not measured: ring-p (the claim that the merge inlet is also the last outlet there comes from reading create:361); N other than 3; LANES other than 2; a writer that is one dd from a file going through a tee; seats that poll their ends instead of draining them all the time; the cause of no cuts at 400000 bytes or at 3000 bytes without fullblock under 4x load; per-chunk timing of the tee itself. p read its end with one bounded `head -c TOTAL` and then parsed that with the length / SEAT / head -c procedure. Those are the same bytes in the same order, since p is the only reader, but p did not parse message by message live. The "idle" runs shared the machine with another session's ICC test runs.
mechanism: n/a
method: n/a
confidence: high

@ file mesh.sh, last written 2026-10-05T01:38:44Z
#!/bin/bash
# mesh.sh SIZE DEPTH FLAG(fb|nofb) NTRIALS TAG [stall]
set -u
S=/home/user/ICC/.claude/skills
W=$(dirname "$(readlink -f "$0")")
nSize=$1; nDepth=$2; eFlag=$3; nTrials=$4; osTag=$5; bStall=${6:-}
osFlag=; [ "$eFlag" = fb ] && osFlag=iflag=fullblock
pT=$(mktemp -d "$W/t.XXXXXX")
aDrain=(); pP=
cleanup() {
  [ ${#aDrain[@]} -eq 0 ] || kill "${aDrain[@]}" 2>/dev/null
  [ -z "$pP" ] || timeout 120 "$S/icc-patch/scripts/remove" "$pP" >/dev/null 2>&1 || echo "REMOVE FAILED $pP"
  wait 2>/dev/null; rm -rf "$pT"
}
trap cleanup EXIT; trap 'exit 1' INT TERM
if [ "$nDepth" = direct ]; then
  # no tee: three inlet pipes and an outlet, one merge; map written here
  pP=$(mktemp -d "$W/direct.XXXXXX"); map=$pP/patch; : > "$map"; aPieces=()
  for i in 0 1 2 o; do
    q=$(timeout 60 "$S/icc-pipes/scripts/create" 2) || { echo "$osTag pipe failed"; exit 1; }
    echo "$q" >> "$W/mine.txt"; aPieces+=("$q")
    if [ $i = o ]; then echo "p 1 $q" >> "$map"; else echo "$i 0 $q" >> "$map"; fi
  done
  m=$(timeout 60 "$S/icc-merge/scripts/create" "${aPieces[3]}" 0 "${aPieces[0]}" "${aPieces[1]}" "${aPieces[2]}") || { echo "$osTag merge failed"; exit 1; }
  echo "$m" >> "$W/mine.txt"
  cleanup() {
    timeout 60 "$S/icc-merge/scripts/remove" "$m" >/dev/null 2>&1 || echo "REMOVE FAILED $m"
    for q in "${aPieces[@]}"; do timeout 60 "$S/icc-pipes/scripts/remove" "$q" >/dev/null 2>&1 || echo "REMOVE FAILED $q"; done
    rm -rf "$pP" "$pT"; wait 2>/dev/null
  }
else
  pP=$(timeout 300 "$S/icc-patch/scripts/create" mesh-p 3 2 "$nDepth") || { echo "$osTag create failed"; exit 1; }
  echo "$pP" >> "$W/mine.txt"
  map=$pP/patch
fi
pRead=$(awk '$1=="p" && $2==1 {print $3}' "$map")
aWE=(); aBody=()
for i in 0 1 2; do
  aWE+=("$(awk -v s=$i '$1==s && $2==0 {print $3}' "$map")")
  osAbc=abc; aBody+=("$(head -c "$nSize" /dev/zero | tr '\0' "${osAbc:i:1}")")
done
for i in 0 1 2; do
  [ -n "$bStall" ] && [ $i -eq 2 ] && continue
  for e in $(awk -v s=$i '$1==s && $2==1 {print $3}' "$map"); do
    timeout 7200 dd if="$e/0" of=/dev/null bs=65536 status=none & aDrain+=($!)
  done
done
aS2=($(awk '$1==2 && $2==1 {print $3}' "$map"))
nMsg=$(( ${#nSize} + 1 + 7 + nSize )); nTotal=$((3 * nMsg))
mkfifo "$pT/bar" "$pT/rdy"
for ((k = 1; k <= nTrials; k++)); do
  exec 8<> "$pT/bar" 7<> "$pT/rdy"
  aSeat=()
  for i in 0 1 2; do
    (
      exec 9< "$pT/bar"
      printf r >&7
      read -N 1 -u 9
      exec 9<&- 8>&- 7>&-
      printf '%s\nSEAT %s\n%s' "$nSize" "$i" "${aBody[i]}" |
        timeout 60 dd of="${aWE[i]}/0" bs=4096 $osFlag status=none
    ) & aSeat+=($!)
  done
  timeout 60 head -c "$nTotal" < "$pRead/0" > "$pT/out" & pidP=$!
  read -N 3 -t 60 -u 7 osR || echo "$osTag t=$k READY-TIMEOUT"
  t0=$(date +%s%N)
  printf xyz >&8
  if [ -n "$bStall" ]; then
    ( sleep 1; for e in "${aS2[@]}"; do timeout 30 head -c "$nMsg" < "$e/0" > /dev/null & done; wait ) & pidS2=$!
  fi
  wait "$pidP"; eP=$?
  t1=$(date +%s%N)
  eW=0; for p in "${aSeat[@]}"; do wait "$p" || eW=$?; done
  [ -z "$bStall" ] || wait "$pidS2"
  exec 8>&- 7>&-
  exec {fdL}< "$pRead/0"; osLeft=; read -t 0 -u $fdL && osLeft=LEFTOVER; exec {fdL}<&-
  echo "$osTag size=$nSize d=$nDepth $eFlag t=$k ms=$(( (t1 - t0) / 1000000 )) eP=$eP eW=$eW $osLeft $("$W/an.py" "$nSize" "$pT/out")"
done
@ file an.py, last written 2026-10-05T01:38:26Z
#!/usr/bin/env python3
# an.py SIZE FILE: tokenize p's stream; print CLASS and a token summary
import sys
size = int(sys.argv[1]); d = open(sys.argv[2], 'rb').read()
total = 3 * (len(str(size)) + 1 + 7 + size)
toks = []; i = 0; n = len(d)
while i < n:
    c = d[i]
    if c in b'abc':
        j = i
        while j < n and d[j] == c: j += 1
        toks.append((chr(c), j - i)); i = j
    elif d[i:i+5] == b'SEAT ' and i + 6 < n and d[i+6] == 10:
        toks.append(('S' + chr(d[i+5]), 0)); i += 7
    elif 48 <= c <= 57:
        j = i
        while j < n and 48 <= d[j] <= 57: j += 1
        if j < n and d[j] == 10:
            toks.append(('L' + d[i:j].decode(), 0)); i = j + 1
        else:
            toks.append(('X', j - i)); i = j
    else:
        toks.append(('X', 1)); i += 1
summ = ' '.join(t if k == 0 else '%s%d' % (t, k) for t, k in toks)
want = []
ok = False
# expected: three messages, each L S<k> body(k)
if len(toks) == 9:
    seen = set(); ok = True
    for m in range(3):
        L, S, B = toks[3*m:3*m+3]
        if L[0] != 'L%d' % size or not S[0].startswith('S'): ok = False; break
        k = S[0][1]
        if k not in '012' or B != ('abc'[int(k)], size) or k in seen: ok = False; break
        seen.add(k)
cnt = {ch: sum(k for t, k in toks if t == ch) for ch in 'abc'}
nL = sum(1 for t, k in toks if t == 'L%d' % size)
nS = sum(1 for t, k in toks if t.startswith('S'))
if ok: cls = 'OK'
elif len(d) < total: cls = 'SHORT'
elif all(cnt[ch] == size for ch in 'abc') and nL == 3 and nS == 3 and len(d) == total: cls = 'CUT'
else: cls = 'BAD'
print(cls, len(d), summ if cls != 'OK' else '')
@ file drive.sh, last written 2026-10-05T01:39:08Z
#!/bin/bash
# drive.sh NYES PLANFILE LOG: start NYES busy loops, run each plan line as mesh.sh args, log
set -u
W=$(dirname "$(readlink -f "$0")")
nYes=$1; pPlan=$2; pLog=$3
aYes=()
trap '[ ${#aYes[@]} -eq 0 ] || kill "${aYes[@]}" 2>/dev/null; wait 2>/dev/null' EXIT
trap 'exit 1' INT TERM
for ((y = 0; y < nYes; y++)); do timeout 7200 yes > /dev/null & aYes+=($!); done
echo "${aYes[*]}" > "$W/yespids.$$"
sleep 3
while read -r osLine; do
  [ -n "$osLine" ] || continue
  echo "# $(date +%T) load=$(cut -d' ' -f1-3 /proc/loadavg) nyes=$nYes run: $osLine" >> "$pLog"
  timeout 3000 "$W/mesh.sh" $osLine >> "$pLog" 2>&1
done < "$pPlan"
echo "# done $(date +%T)" >> "$pLog"
@ file hcheck.sh, last written 2026-10-05T02:00:12Z
#!/bin/bash
# the proposed tests/patch.sh check, standalone, with the harness's helpers copied
set -eu
pIccPatch=/home/user/ICC/.claude/skills/icc-patch/scripts
W=$(dirname "$(readlink -f "$0")")
pWork=$(mktemp -d "$W/h.XXXXXX")
pDir=
trap '[ -z "$pDir" ] || [ ! -d "$pDir" ] || "$pIccPatch/remove" "$pDir" >/dev/null 2>&1; rm -rf "$pWork"' EXIT
pEnd() { awk -v s="$1" -v i="$2" -v p="$3" '$1==s && $2==i && ("," $4 ",") ~ ("," p ",") {print $3}' "$pDir/patch"; }
pAt() { local f; f=$(pEnd "$1" "$2" "$3"); [ -n "$f" ] || { echo "no end: $*" >&2; exit 1; }; echo "$f"; }
vMake() { pDir=$("$pIccPatch/create" "$@"); echo "$pDir" >> "$W/mine.txt"; }
vMake mesh-p 3
aJobs=()
for osSeat in 0 1; do
  pWriteEnd=$(pAt $osSeat 0 p)
  head -c 100000 /dev/zero | tr '\0' "$osSeat" |
    timeout 10 dd of="$pWriteEnd/0" bs=4096 2>/dev/null &
  aJobs+=($!)
done
pReadEnd=$(pAt 0 1 1)
timeout 10 dd if="$pReadEnd/0" of=/dev/null bs=4096 2>/dev/null &
aJobs+=($!)
pReadEnd=$(pAt 1 1 0)
timeout 10 dd if="$pReadEnd/0" of=/dev/null bs=4096 2>/dev/null &
aJobs+=($!)
pReadEnd=$(pAt p 1 0)
timeout 2 head -c 200000 "$pReadEnd/0" > "$pWork/met" || :
osFirst=$(head -c 1 "$pWork/met")
nFirst=$(tr -cd "$osFirst" < "$pWork/met" | wc -c); nOther=$(tr -d "$osFirst" < "$pWork/met" | wc -c)
echo "first=$osFirst nFirst=$nFirst nOther=$nOther"
[ "$nFirst" -lt 100000 ]
[ "$nOther" -gt 0 ]
"$pIccPatch/remove" "$pDir"
wait "${aJobs[@]}" || :
echo PASS

@ report the checking agent
question: Re-reading the copier code: does the mechanism they give hold, and is the 'certain' case really certain?
answer: The mechanism holds. One correction to the certain case: the tee's read of a write of at most PIPE_BUF can also take whatever the seat wrote after it. That makes the tee's write to the inlet longer than PIPE_BUF, and then it is not atomic. It lands whole only if the inlet has room for all of it. 'Lands in the inlet whole' is certain only when the tee read that write alone. That was always true in both of our setups, since each seat wrote one message per trial. Measured with strace (coreutils tee 9.4, `tee -a o1 o2 < fifo`, fed 20000 bytes by `dd bs=4096`): read(0, 8192), write(1), write(3), write(4), then read again, three rounds and a 3616-byte tail. bash 5.2.21 `printf "%s\nSEAT %s\n%s" 20000 0 "$body"` wrote 6, then 7, then 4096-byte chunks. In that same run the dd without fullblock wrote 4096 first: its first read took both header lines and 4083 body bytes, because printf had written them all already. So dd without fullblock CAN pass the header lines on alone, but does not always.
mechanism: icc-tee/scripts/create:114 `exec tee -a -- "${aOuts[@]}" < "$pSrc/$iLane" 3<&-`. icc-patch/scripts/create:307-311 put each other seat's cable head into osOutlets, 312-315 add the merge inlet's head last, and 319 runs `vTee "$pWrite" 0 $osOutlets`. On ring-p, create:361 is `vTee "$pSendTail" 0 "$pRecvHead" "$pToParentHead"`, so there too the inlet is last. icc-merge/scripts/create:133-154 loop while bMore. 137 is `read -t 0 -u "$fdIn" || continue`. 144 is `osStats=$(exec dd bs=4096 count=1 <&"$fdIn" 2>&1 >&"${aOuts[iPer]}")`. 151 is `bMore=1`, reached only after a block moved. 156 is `[ "$bIdle" -eq 0 ] || read -t 0.05 -u "$fdIdle" || :`. The merge leaves a SRC the first time it finds the inlet empty. The tee fills the inlet only after it has written every cable. The not-atomic case comes from Linux pipe_write, not ICC code: a write over PIPE_BUF can put its first part into the pipe's last page and then wait for a free slot while the pipe is full. I did not measure that case.
method: Read the scripts above. Ran `strace -f -e trace=write bash -c 'printf ... | dd of=/dev/null bs=4096 status=none'` and `strace -e trace=read,write tee -a o1 o2 < fifo` once each, in /tmp/claude-0/-home-user/c19ad2bc-6d47-58bd-bbd3-147cbec3972c/scratchpad/measure/v.7Ora13 (pf.tr, tee.tr).
confidence: high

question: With every seat reading and four busy loops per CPU, do I get their cuts at 8000-20000 bytes, and none on the other side of the PIPE_BUF line (3000 bytes)?
answer: Yes. On mesh-p 3 2 1 under 16 `yes` on 4 CPUs, 20000 bytes: cut 3 of 150 with iflag=fullblock and 6 of 300 without. 8000 bytes: 1 of 50 with, 1 of 50 without. 100000 bytes at DEPTH 2, without: 0 of 50. 400000 bytes at DEPTH 6: 0 of 50 (26 with, 24 without). 3000 bytes: 0 of 150 with fullblock, which the code makes certain, and 0 of 50 without. All 11 cuts came exactly after the cut message's first 4096 bytes: 13 header + 4083 body at 20000, 12 + 4084 at 8000. No SHORT, BAD, hang, nonzero exit or leftover bytes on p's end in any trial. Under this load a 20000-byte trial took a median 276-292 ms for 15 merge blocks, about 18 ms per block. 400000-byte trials took a median 4.5 s, max 5.2 s.
mechanism: It is the same window at the first block. When the merge takes the first block, the next chunk has not yet reached the inlet (merge create:137, 151). Nothing in the code explains why 400000 bytes never cut, and I did not trace it.
method: W=/tmp/claude-0/-home-user/c19ad2bc-6d47-58bd-bbd3-147cbec3972c/scratchpad/measure/v.7Ora13. I ran `$W/drive.sh 16 plan4x.txt log4x.txt`, then `$W/drive.sh 16 plan4x2.txt log4x2.txt`. drive.sh starts `timeout 7200 yes >/dev/null` 16 times and kills them on EXIT. Each plan line runs `mesh.sh SIZE DEPTH fb|nofb N TAG`, and each call of mesh.sh is one `icc-patch/scripts/create mesh-p 3 2 DEPTH` with N (12-25) trials on it. Conditions alternated in rounds. Every read end of every seat was drained all along by `timeout 7200 dd if=END/0 of=/dev/null bs=65536 status=none`. In each trial, each seat subshell wrote a ready byte to one fifo and blocked in `read -N 1` on a barrier fifo. Main waited for 3 ready bytes, then wrote 3 bytes to the barrier. Each seat then ran `printf '%s\nSEAT %s\n%s' SIZE i BODY | timeout 60 dd of=WRITE_END/0 bs=4096 [iflag=fullblock] status=none`, with BODY a, b or c repeated. p ran `timeout 60 head -c TOTAL < P/0`, started before the release. an.py tokenized the stream into length lines, SEAT lines and runs of a/b/c, and classed it OK, CUT, SHORT or BAD. After each trial `read -t 0` on p's lane checked for leftover bytes. The 1-minute loadavg at run starts was 0.24 on the first call, before it caught up, then 7.9-18.3.
confidence: high

question: Is it true that 'each time another seat's whole message, or both others', came out inside the cut one'?
answer: No, not in every case. In 10 of my 11 cut trials it was so. In one trial (8000 bytes, fullblock, t=8) the stream was `L S1 b4084 | L S0 a4084 | b3916 | L S2 c8000 | a3916`. Seat 1's message had only seat 0's first 4096 bytes inside it, which is not a whole message. Seat 0's message had the rest of seat 1's and all of seat 2's inside it. Two seats were each cut inside the other.
mechanism: The merge leaves inlet 1 after one block and finds inlet 0 holding only its first block. It takes that and leaves inlet 0 too (merge create:137, 151), because both seats were still in their first-block gap. Nothing in the code requires what lands inside a cut to be whole.
method: Same runs as above. log4x2.txt line `L4x-8000-fb size=8000 d=1 fb t=8 ... CUT 24036 L8000 S1 b4084 L8000 S0 a4084 b3916 L8000 S2 c8000 a3916`.
confidence: high

question: Is it the tee? The control with no tee under the same load.
answer: It holds up. With no tee (dd writing straight into a merge's three inlets), 20000 bytes were cut 0 of 300 times (72 with fullblock, 228 without). The mesh-p calls alternating with them in the same two runs were cut 9 of 450 times. One-sided Fisher exact p = 0.0098. Their own result was 0 of 300 against 6 of 300. Why the tee opens the window (their wake-up inference) I did not trace either.
mechanism: A tee stands between the seat's dd and the inlet, and tee(1) blocks in read (tee create:114). It copies each chunk to the cables before the inlet (patch create:307-319). With no tee, dd's writes go straight into the inlet that the merge polls.
method: `mesh.sh 20000 direct fb|nofb N TAG` creates 4 pipes with `icc-pipes/scripts/create 2` and runs `icc-merge/scripts/create OUT 0 IN0 IN1 IN2`, with the same barrier, writer, p reader and an.py. It alternated with `mesh.sh 20000 1 ...` calls in plan4x.txt (6 rounds) and plan4x2.txt (6 rounds of 25 direct + 25 mesh), all under 16 `yes`.
confidence: medium

question: The stalled-seat case: DEPTH 1 against DEPTH 2 at 100000 bytes.
answer: Confirmed, with one number corrected. At mesh-p 3 2 1, all three seats wrote 100000 bytes at once while seat 2 read nothing for 1 s and then read. 20 of 20 trials were cut (10 with fullblock, 10 without), in 1054-1103 ms. Seat 2's message came out whole each time, between the first and second parts of the other two. The first parts of seats 0 and 1 were 61440 or 65536 bytes with the header, as they say, but also 61454 bytes (body 61440 + 14) in 4 of the 20 parts without fullblock. 'The first 61440 or 65536 bytes' is not the whole range. At DEPTH 2, 20 of 20 came out whole in 169-225 ms, before seat 2 read.
mechanism: The tee blocks writing seat 2's full cable, and that outlet comes before the inlet (patch create:307-319). The inlet gets nothing more, the merge empties it, and it moves on (merge create:137, 151). Patch SKILL.md:178: 'A tee waits on its fullest outlet'. How many bytes fit in 16 pages depends on how the tee's chunks fall across them, which is why the size varies.
method: `$W/drive.sh 0 plan0.txt log0.txt` with plan lines `100000 {1,2} {fb,nofb} 10 TAG stall`. In stall mode seat 2's two ends are not drained. Each trial runs `(sleep 1; timeout 30 head -c MSG < END/0)` for each of seat 2's two ends, so it takes exactly the one message each end carries. No busy loops ran. The 1-minute loadavg at run starts was 6.7-16.5, still falling from the run before.
confidence: high

question: No load added, and the proposed harness check.
answer: With no busy loops, 20000 bytes without fullblock came out whole 50 of 50 times, and 8000 bytes with it 50 of 50, on mesh-p 3 2 1. Their 'idle' wording is wrong as stated: by their own account another session's ICC tests were running, with loadavg 0.18-2.12. It should read 'no load added'. The proposed check passed 10 of 10 with no load added and 5 of 5 under 16 `yes`. The first seat's byte count at p was always 61440 or 65536, and the other seat's 57344 to 65536. The first assertion is certain from the code, since tee 0 cannot pass the inlet more than seat 2's one-pipe cable takes. The second rests on a 2 s bound for about 32 merge blocks. Under 4x load I measured about 18 ms per block, so about 0.6 s.
mechanism: patch create:307-319: the cables come before the inlet. At DEPTH 1 seat 2's cable from each tee is one pipe (vSeries tail, nPipes=1, create:223-241).
method: $W/hcheck.sh is the proposed snippet run standalone, with pEnd, pAt and vMake copied from tests/patch.sh:49-56, 73-84 and 121-124. It was run 10 times, then 5 times while 16 `timeout 600 yes >/dev/null` ran.
confidence: high

question: What I did not measure
answer: Not measured: the one-busy-loop-per-CPU condition (their 800/800 and 1399/1400); their 1000-trial no-load sweep (I ran only 100 trials with no load added); ring-p (the claim that the inlet is the last outlet comes from create:361); N other than 3; LANES other than 2; the not-atomic case where the tee's read holds a write of at most PIPE_BUF plus what followed it while the inlet is full; why 400000 bytes and 3000 bytes without fullblock never cut; the scheduler-level reason the tee opens the window. Leftovers: after all runs, the list scripts of patch, merge, tee and pipes showed none of the 154 pieces logged in $W/mine.txt. No `yes` and no helper process of mine was alive. `git -C /home/user/ICC status --short` was empty.
mechanism: n/a
method: n/a
confidence: high
